\displaystyle\geq\overline{c}_{\rho}\cdot\log(1+x)-\log\left(1+\frac{\overline{c}_{\rho}}{2}\cdot\log(1+x)\right)+\frac{c^{\dagger}_{\rho}}{2}\cdot\log\left(\frac{c^{\dagger}_{\rho}\wedge\overline{c}_{\rho}}{4}\cdot\log(1+x)\right)\cdot\log(1+x)

(B.84)

\displaystyle\geq\overline{c}_{\rho}\cdot\log(1+x)-\frac{\overline{c}_{\rho}}{2}\cdot\log(1+x)+\frac{c^{\dagger}_{\rho}}{2}\cdot\log\left(\frac{\log 2}{2}\cdot c^{\dagger}_{\rho}\wedge\overline{c}_{\rho}\right)\cdot\log(1+x)

(B.85)

\displaystyle\geq\overline{c}_{\rho}\cdot\log(1+x)-\frac{\overline{c}_{\rho}}{2}\cdot\log(1+x)-\frac{\overline{c}_{\rho}}{2}\cdot\log(1+x)=0,

(B.86)

where the first inequality plugs in the choice of
u
, the second inequality uses the inequality that
\log(1+x)\leq x
for
x>-1
, and the fact that
x\geq 1
, the last inequality uses the property (
B.83
) that the constant
c_{\rho}^{\dagger}
satisfies.
Therefore, by taking
u=(c^{\dagger}_{\rho}\wedge\overline{c}_{\rho})/2\cdot\log(1+x)
, the associated
\lambda
satisfies
\partial h_{\bm{v},\bm{r},S}(\lambda)\geq 0
.
Therefore, using the reparametrization formula (
B.80
), we get back to the desired
\underline{\lambda}
as

\displaystyle\underline{\lambda}:=\frac{r_{\max}}{\log\left(\frac{x}{u}\right)}=\frac{r_{\max}}{\log\left(\frac{x}{\frac{1}{2}\cdot c^{\dagger}_{\rho}\wedge\overline{c}_{\rho}\cdot\log(1+x)}\right)}.

(B.87)

Consequently, we can conclude that the optimal dual variable is lower bounded by

\displaystyle\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S)\geq\underline{\lambda}\geq\frac{r_{\max}}{\log\left(\frac{x}{\frac{1}{2}\cdot c^{\dagger}_{\rho}\wedge\overline{c}_{\rho}\cdot\log(1+x)}\right)}\geq\frac{r_{\max}}{\log\left(\frac{2}{\frac{1}{2}\cdot c^{\dagger}_{\rho}\wedge\overline{c}_{\rho}\cdot\log 2}\right)},

(B.88)

where the third inequality uses the fact that
x\in[1,2]
.
Finally, combining (
B.79
) and (
B.88
), we can conclude that for any
(\bm{v},\bm{r};S)\in\mathcal{V}\times 2^{[4K]}
with
|S|=K
,

\displaystyle\frac{r_{\max}}{\log\left(\frac{2}{\frac{1}{2}\cdot c^{\dagger}_{\rho}\wedge\overline{c}_{\rho}\cdot\log 2}\right)}\leq\lambda_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}^{\star}(S)\leq\frac{r_{\max}}{\underline{c}_{\rho}\cdot\log 2}.

(B.89)

For a quick sanity check of the above result, we remind the reader that the above range of
\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S)
is a valid range since

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{2}{\frac{1}{2}\cdot c^{\dagger}_{\rho}\wedge\overline{c}_{\rho}\cdot\log 2}\right)\geq\underline{c}_{\rho}\cdot\log\left(\frac{2}{\frac{1}{2}\cdot c^{\dagger}_{\rho}\wedge\overline{c}_{\rho}\cdot\log 2}\right)\geq\underline{c}_{\rho}\cdot\log 2.

(B.90)

Step 2.2: lower bound the minimax rate of the quantity
\Delta(S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}},\pi(\mathbb{D}))
.

To lower bound the minimax rate of
\Delta(S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}},\pi(\mathbb{D}))
, it suffices to apply Fano’s Lemma and carefully upper bound the KL divergence between the product choice distributions induced by different instances
(\bm{v},\bm{r}),(\bm{v}^{\prime},\bm{r}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{V}
.
We conclude the results in the following lemma.

Lemma B.4

(Minimax lower bound 1 of
\Delta(S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}},\pi(\mathbb{D}))
)
.

Under the settings inside the proof of Theorem
5.2
for general non-uniform revenue setting, by taking the parameter
\epsilon=\sqrt{C_{1}/80n_{\min}}
where constant
C_{1}>0
is defined in Lemma
B.2
, then there is another absolute constant
C_{2}>0
such that for
K\geq C_{2}
it holds that

\displaystyle\inf_{\pi(\cdot):(2^{[N]}\times[N])^{n}\mapsto 2^{[N]}}\sup_{(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{D}\sim\otimes_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{v}}(\cdot|S_{k})}\big[\Delta(S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}},\pi(\mathbb{D}))\big]\geq\frac{1}{128}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{C_{1}}{5}}\cdot\frac{K}{\epsilon\cdot\sqrt{n_{\min}}},

(B.91)

where the expectation is taken w.r.t. the randomness in the item choices
\{i_{k}\}_{k=1}^{n}
in the observational data.

Proof of Lemma
B.4
.

To prove the minimax rate for the difference
\Delta(S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}},\pi(\mathbb{D}))
, we invoke the Fano’s lemma (Lemma
D.8
).
Specifically, in Lemma
D.8
, if we choose
\Theta:=2^{[N]}
,
\Gamma:=\{S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}:(\bm{r},\bm{v})\in\mathcal{V}\}
,
\rho(\cdot,\cdot):=\Delta(\cdot,\cdot)
, then by the definition of
\mathcal{V}
and Lemma
B.2
,
\Gamma
is
2\delta_{0}
-separated with
\delta_{0}=K/8
.
We define the distribution associated with each element in
\Gamma
as

\mathbb{P}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(\cdot):=\bigotimes_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{v}}(\cdot|S_{k})=\bigotimes_{\ell=1}^{4K}\bigotimes_{j=1}^{n_{\min}}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{v}}(\cdot|S_{j}^{(\ell)}),\quad\text{where}\quad S_{j}^{(\ell)}=S_{(\ell-1)\cdot n_{\min}+j}.

Since we have that
\log M:=\log|\Gamma|=\log\lvert\mathcal{V}\rvert\geq C_{1}K
for some absolute constant
C_{1}
(Lemma
B.2
), Lemma
D.8
implies that when
K\geq C_{2}:=\max\{C_{0},C_{1}^{-1}2\log 2\}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\inf_{\pi(\cdot):(2^{[N]}\times[N])^{n}\mapsto 2^{[N]}}\sup_{(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{D}\sim\mathbb{P}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(\cdot)}\big[\Delta(S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}},\pi(\mathbb{D}))\big]\geq\frac{K}{8}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\sum_{(\bm{r},\bm{v})\in\mathcal{V},(\bm{r}^{\prime},\bm{v}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{V}}D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\mathbb{P}_{\bm{r},\bm{v}}\lVert\mathbb{P}_{\bm{r}^{\prime},\bm{v}^{\prime}})}{C_{1}K\lvert\mathcal{V}\rvert^{2}}\right).

It then remains to establish an upper bound for the summation of KL divergence terms.
To this end, consider that for any pair
(\bm{r},\bm{v}),(\bm{r}^{\prime},\bm{v}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{V}
, we have that

\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\mathbb{P}_{\bm{r},\bm{v}}\lVert\mathbb{P}_{\bm{r}^{\prime},\bm{v}^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=D_{\mathrm{KL}}\left(\bigotimes_{\ell=1}^{4K}\bigotimes_{j=1}^{n_{\min}}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{v}}(\cdot|S_{j}^{(\ell)})\middle\|\bigotimes_{\ell=1}^{4K}\bigotimes_{j=1}^{n_{\min}}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{v}^{\prime}}(\cdot|S_{j}^{(\ell)})\right)

(B.92)

\displaystyle=n_{\min}\sum_{\ell=1}^{4K}D_{\mathrm{KL}}\left(\mathbb{P}_{\bm{v}}(\cdot|S_{j}^{(\ell)})\middle\|\mathbb{P}_{\bm{v}^{\prime}}(\cdot|S_{j}^{(\ell)})\right).

(B.93)

We then have the following result to upper bound each single KL divergence term in (
B.93
).

Lemma B.5

(KL divergence bound 1)
.

For every
\ell\in[4K]
and any pair
(\bm{r},\bm{v}),(\bm{r}^{\prime},\bm{v}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{V}
, it holds that

\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{KL}}\left(\mathbb{P}_{\bm{v}}(\cdot|S_{j}^{(\ell)})\middle\|\mathbb{P}_{\bm{v}^{\prime}}(\cdot|S_{j}^{(\ell)})\right)\leq\begin{cases}5\epsilon^{2},&\text{if }\ell\in(S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(\bm{r},\bm{v})\cup S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(\bm{r}^{\prime},\bm{v}^{\prime}))\setminus(S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(\bm{r},\bm{v})\cap S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(\bm{r}^{\prime},\bm{v}^{\prime})),\\
0,&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Proof of Lemma
B.5
.

Please refer to Lemma 5.4 in
Han et al. 2025
for a detailed proof.
∎

Therefore, with (
B.93
) and Lemma
B.5
, we can finally obtain that

\displaystyle\inf_{\pi(\cdot):(2^{[N]}\times[N])^{n}\mapsto 2^{[N]}}\sup_{(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{D}\sim\mathbb{P}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(\cdot)}\big[\Delta(S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}},\pi(\mathbb{D}))\big]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{K}{8}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\max_{(\bm{r},\bm{v})\in\mathcal{V},(\bm{r}^{\prime},\bm{v}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{V}}D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\mathbb{P}_{\bm{r},\bm{v}}\lVert\mathbb{P}_{\bm{r}^{\prime},\bm{v}^{\prime}})}{C_{1}K}\right)

(B.94)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{K}{8}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{n_{\min}\cdot 4K\cdot 5\epsilon^{2}}{C_{1}K}\right)

(B.95)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{K}{32}=\frac{1}{128}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{C_{1}}{5}}\cdot\frac{K}{\epsilon\cdot\sqrt{n_{\min}}},

(B.96)

where the second inequality uses Lemma
B.5
and (
B.93
), while the last inequality and the last equality both use the choice of
\epsilon
that
\epsilon=\sqrt{C_{1}/80n_{\min}}
.
This completes the proof of Lemma
B.4
.
∎

Step 2.3: finishing the proof.

With all the preparations above, we can lower bound the minimax lower bound of the expected suboptimality gap by

\displaystyle\inf_{\pi(\cdot):(2^{[N]}\times[N])^{n}\mapsto 2^{[N]}}\sup_{(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{D}\sim\otimes_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{v}}(\cdot|S_{k})}\big[\mathrm{SupOpt}_{\rho}(\pi(\mathbb{D});\bm{v},\bm{r})\big]

(B.97)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\inf_{\pi(\cdot):(2^{[N]}\times[N])^{n}\mapsto 2^{[N]}}\sup_{(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{D}\sim\otimes_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{v}}(\cdot|S_{k})}\big[\mathrm{SupOpt}_{\rho}(\widetilde{S}(\pi(\mathbb{D}));\bm{v},\bm{r})\big]

(B.98)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\inf_{\pi(\cdot):(2^{[N]}\times[N])^{n}\mapsto 2^{[N]}}\sup_{(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}}\bigg\{\frac{\epsilon}{18}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{D}\sim\otimes_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{v}}(\cdot|S_{k})}\Big[\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(\widetilde{S}(\pi(\mathbb{D})))

(B.99)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\Big(1-\exp\big(-r_{\max}/\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(\widetilde{S}(\pi(\mathbb{D})))\big)\Big)\cdot\Delta(S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}},\widetilde{S}(\pi(\mathbb{D})))\Big]\bigg\}

(B.100)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\frac{\epsilon}{18}\cdot\inf_{S\subseteq[4K],|S|=K}\sup_{(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}}\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S)\cdot\big(1-\exp(-r_{\max}/\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S))\big)

(B.101)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\inf_{\pi(\cdot):(2^{[N]}\times[N])^{n}\mapsto 2^{[N]}}\sup_{(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{D}\sim\otimes_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{v}}(\cdot|S_{k})}\big[\Delta(S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}},\pi(\mathbb{D}))\big]

(B.102)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\frac{1}{2304}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{C_{1}}{5}}\cdot\big(1-2^{-\underline{c}_{\rho}}\big)\cdot\frac{r_{\max}}{\log\left(\frac{2}{\frac{1}{2}\cdot c^{\dagger}_{\rho}\wedge\overline{c}_{\rho}\cdot\log 2}\right)}\cdot\frac{K}{\sqrt{n_{\min}}}.

(B.103)

where the first inequality uses (
B.58
) to reduce any potential output
\pi(\mathbb{D})
into an assortment
\widetilde{S}(\pi(\mathbb{D}))\subseteq[4K]
with size
K
, the second inequality uses inequality (
B.66
),
the third inequality uses the property of
\widetilde{S}(\pi(\mathbb{D}))
and the fact that

\displaystyle\inf_{\pi(\cdot):(2^{[N]}\times[N])^{n}\mapsto 2^{[N]}}\sup_{(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{D}\sim\otimes_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{v}}(\cdot|S_{k})}\big[\Delta(S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}},\widetilde{S}(\pi(\mathbb{D})))\big]

(B.104)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\inf_{\pi(\cdot):(2^{[N]}\times[N])^{n}\mapsto 2^{[N]}}\sup_{(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{D}\sim\otimes_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{v}}(\cdot|S_{k})}\big[\Delta(S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}},\pi(\mathbb{D}))\big],

(B.105)

the last inequality uses the upper and lower bounds of the optimal dual variable
\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S)
for any assortment
S\subseteq[4K]
with size
|S|=K
and any instance
(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}
(see (
B.89
) in
Step 2.1
), together with Lemma
B.4
.
Now using the fact that
n_{\min}=\min_{i\in S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}}n_{i}
according to our choice of the dataset
\mathbb{D}
, we can
complete the proof of Theorem
5.2
for the general non-uniform revenue case.
∎

Proof of Theorem
5.2
: uniform revenue case
.

The proofs for the uniform revenue case can follow a similar approach to the general non-uniform revenue case, but it requires a slightly different construction of the hard instance class to ensure the condition of uniform revenue.
More specifically, we still rely on the division rule (
B.47
).
Then given any division
(\mathcal{N}^{\star},\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}},\mathcal{N}^{0})
satisfying (
B.47
), we define an instance
(\bm{v},\bm{r})
by

\displaystyle\big(\bm{v}_{\mathcal{N}^{\star}},\bm{r}_{\mathcal{N}^{\star}}\big):=\left(\frac{1}{K}+\epsilon^{\prime},r_{\max}\right),\quad\big(\bm{v}_{\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}}},\bm{r}_{\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}}}\big)=\left(\frac{1}{K},r_{\max}\right),\quad\big(\bm{v}_{\mathcal{N}^{0}},\bm{r}_{\mathcal{N}^{0}}\big)=\left(\frac{1}{K},r_{\max}\right),

(B.106)

where we choose the parameter
\epsilon^{\prime}
by the following,

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\prime}:=\sqrt{\frac{C_{1}}{80Kn_{\min}}}.

(B.107)

with the constant
C_{1}
specified in Lemma
B.2
.
Now we construct the hard instance class
\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{uni}}
as following,

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{uni}}:=\Big\{\text{$(\bm{v},\bm{r})$ induced by $(\mathcal{N}^{\star},\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}},\mathcal{N}^{0})$ via \eqref{eq:_proof_lower_bound_2}}\,\Big|\,\text{$\mathcal{N}^{\star}\in\mathcal{F}$ with $\mathcal{F}$ given by Lemma~\ref{lem-packing-number}}\Big\}.

(B.108)

We have the following result for the optimal robust assortment associated with instances in the class
\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{uni}}
.

Lemma B.6

(Optimal robust assortment for class (
B.108
))
.

For any problem instance
(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{uni}}
associated with the division
(\mathcal{N}^{\star},\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}},\mathcal{N}^{0})
, its optimal robust assortment
S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}
is given by
S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}=\mathcal{N}^{\star}
.

Proof of Lemma
B.6
.

By definition, for any instance
(\bm{v},\bm{r})
with partitions given by
(\mathcal{N}^{\star},\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}},\mathcal{N}^{0})
, the robust expected revenue of any assortment
S
is given by

\displaystyle R_{\rho}(S;\bm{v},\bm{r})

\displaystyle=\sup_{\lambda\geq 0}\left\{-\lambda\cdot\log\left(\frac{1+e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}\cdot(1/K\cdot(|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}}|+|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|)+(1/K+\epsilon^{\prime})\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|)}{1+1/K\cdot(|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}}|+|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|)+(1/K+\epsilon^{\prime})\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|)}\right)-\lambda\rho\right\}

(B.109)

\displaystyle=\sup_{\lambda\geq 0}\left\{-\lambda\cdot\log\left(e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}+\frac{1-e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}}{1+1/K\cdot(|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}}|+|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|)+(1/K+\epsilon^{\prime})\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|)}\right)-\lambda\rho\right\}

(B.110)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\lambda\geq 0}\left\{-\lambda\cdot\log\left(e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}+\frac{1-e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}}{1+\left(\frac{1}{K}+\epsilon^{\prime}\right)\cdot|\mathcal{N}^{\star}|}\right)-\lambda\rho\right\},

(B.111)

where the last inequality becomes equality only when the assortment
S=\mathcal{N}^{\star}
. This proves Lemma
B.6
.
∎

Therefore, Lemma
B.6
tells us that under the new hard instance class
\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{uni}}
, the optimal robust assortment of an instance associated with division
(\mathcal{N}^{\star},\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}},\mathcal{N}^{0})
is still
S_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}^{\star}=\mathcal{N}^{\star}
.
In view of this, the rest of the proofs for the uniform case align well with the proofs for the general non-uniform case, with exceptional difference we discuss in detail in the sequel.

First of all, we construct the observational dataset
\mathbb{D}
in the same way as in (
B.55
).
Then we can lower bound the suboptimality gap of any assortment
S\subseteq[N]
with
|S|\leq K
as following.
Noting that for any such assortment
S
, we can upper bound its robust expected revenue via

\displaystyle R_{\rho}(S;\bm{v},\bm{r})

\displaystyle=\sup_{\lambda\geq 0}\left\{-\lambda\cdot\log\left(e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}+\frac{1-e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}}{1+1/K\cdot(|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}}|+|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|)+(1/K+\epsilon^{\prime})\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|)}\right)-\lambda\rho\right\}

(B.112)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\lambda\geq 0}\left\{-\lambda\cdot\log\left(e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}+\frac{1-e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}}{1+1/K\cdot(K-|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|)+(1/K+\epsilon^{\prime})\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|)}\right)-\lambda\rho\right\}

(B.113)

\displaystyle=R_{\rho}(\widetilde{S}(S);\bm{v},\bm{r}),\quad\text{where}\quad\widetilde{S}(S):=(S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star})\cup\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}}_{K-|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|}\subseteq\mathcal{N}^{\star}\cup\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}},

(B.114)

where
(\mathcal{N}^{\star},\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}},\mathcal{N}^{0})
is the division associated with the instance
(\bm{v},\bm{r})
, and
\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}}_{K-|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|}
is any
(K-|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|)
-sized subset of
\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}}
.
That being said, it suffices to consider all assortments
S\subseteq\mathcal{N}^{\star}\cup\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}}=[4K]
with
|S|=K
.
Now for any assortment of this kind and for any instance
(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{uni}}
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathrm{SubOpt}_{\rho}(S;\bm{v},\bm{r})=R_{\rho}(S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}};\bm{v},\bm{r})-R_{\rho}(S;\bm{v},\bm{r})

(B.115)

\displaystyle\qquad=\sup_{\lambda\geq 0}\left\{-\lambda\cdot\log\left(\frac{1+\exp(-r_{\max}/\lambda)\cdot(1+K\epsilon^{\prime})}{2+K\epsilon^{\prime}}\right)-\lambda\rho\right\}

(B.116)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\sup_{\lambda\geq 0}\left\{-\lambda\cdot\log\left(\frac{1+\exp(-r_{\max}/\lambda)\cdot(1+|S\cap S^{\star}|\cdot\epsilon^{\prime})}{2+|S\cap S^{\star}|\cdot\epsilon^{\prime}}\right)-\lambda\rho\right\}

(B.117)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{18}\cdot\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S)\cdot\big(1-\exp(-r_{\max}/\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S))\big)\cdot\Delta(S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}},S),

(B.118)

where the first inequality is from the dual representation of the robust expected revenue, and the inequality follows the exactly the same argument as (
B.66
), since the left hand side of the inequality shares the same form with the left hand side of (
B.66
)!
Here the optimal dual
\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S)
is defined in the same way as (
B.67
), which means that it also enjoys the upper and lower bounds we derived in (
B.89
)
4
4

4

We note that the difference in
\epsilon
and
\epsilon^{\prime}
does not change the conclusion of the proofs for (
B.66
) and the bounds of
\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S)
. Besides
\epsilon^{\prime}
, the difference of the classes
\mathcal{V}
and
\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{uni}}
only lies in the items in
\mathcal{N}^{0}
, and it does not make a difference in proving the bounds of
\lambda_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}^{\star}(S)
for assortments
S\subseteq[4K]=[N]\setminus\mathcal{N}^{0}
.
.
Consequently, it remains to derive the minimax lower bound for the difference
\Delta(S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}},\pi(\mathbb{D}))
for the hard instance class
\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{uni}}
.

Lemma B.7

(Minimax lower bound 2 of
\Delta(S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}},\pi(\mathbb{D}))
)
.

Under the settings inside the proof of Theorem
5.2
for the uniform revenue setting, by taking the parameter
\epsilon=\sqrt{C_{1}/80Kn_{\min}}
where constant
C_{1}>0
is defined in Lemma
B.2
, then there is another absolute constant
C_{2}>0
such that for
K\geq C_{2}
it holds that

\displaystyle\inf_{\pi(\cdot):(2^{[N]}\times[N])^{n}\mapsto 2^{[N]}}\sup_{(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{uni}}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{D}\sim\otimes_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{v}}(\cdot|S_{k})}\big[\Delta(S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}},\pi(\mathbb{D}))\big]\geq\frac{1}{128}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{C_{1}}{5}}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{K}}{\epsilon^{\prime}\cdot\sqrt{n_{\min}}},

(B.119)

where the expectation is taken w.r.t. the randomness in the item choices
\{i_{k}\}_{k=1}^{n}
in the observational data.

Proof of Lemma
B.7
.

It suffices to repeat the proofs for Lemma
B.4
, except by using a different upper bound on KL divergence terms in (
B.93
) in the following lemma.
∎

Lemma B.8

(KL divergence bound 2)
.

For every
\ell\in[4K]
and any pair
(\bm{r},\bm{v}),(\bm{r}^{\prime},\bm{v}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{uni}}
, it holds that

\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{KL}}\left(\mathbb{P}_{\bm{v}}(\cdot|S_{j}^{(\ell)})\middle\|\mathbb{P}_{\bm{v}^{\prime}}(\cdot|S_{j}^{(\ell)})\right)\leq\begin{cases}5K(\epsilon^{\prime})^{2},&\text{if }\ell\in(S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(\bm{r},\bm{v})\cup S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(\bm{r}^{\prime},\bm{v}^{\prime}))\setminus(S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(\bm{r},\bm{v})\cap S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(\bm{r}^{\prime},\bm{v}^{\prime})),\\
0,&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Proof of Lemma
B.8
.

Please refer to Appendix C.2 in
Han et al. 2025
for a detailed proof.
∎

With Lemma
B.8
, we can repeat the proof of Lemma
B.4
to obtain that,

\displaystyle\inf_{\pi(\cdot):(2^{[N]}\times[N])^{n}\mapsto 2^{[N]}}\sup_{(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{uni}}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{D}\sim\mathbb{P}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(\cdot)}\big[\Delta(S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}},\pi(\mathbb{D}))\big]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{K}{8}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\max_{(\bm{r},\bm{v})\in\mathcal{V},(\bm{r}^{\prime},\bm{v}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{V}}D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\mathbb{P}_{\bm{r},\bm{v}}\lVert\mathbb{P}_{\bm{r}^{\prime},\bm{v}^{\prime}})}{C_{1}K}\right)

(B.120)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{K}{8}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{n_{\min}\cdot 4K^{2}\cdot 5(\epsilon^{\prime})^{2}}{C_{1}K}\right)

(B.121)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{K}{32}=\frac{1}{128}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{C_{1}}{5}}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{K}}{\epsilon^{\prime}\cdot\sqrt{n_{\min}}},

(B.122)

where the second inequality uses Lemma
B.8
and (
B.93
), while the last inequality and the last equality both use the choice of
\epsilon^{\prime}
that
\epsilon^{\prime}=\sqrt{C_{1}/80Kn_{\min}}
.
This completes the proof of Lemma
B.7
.

Finally, we can lower bound the minimax lower bound of the expected suboptimality gap by

\displaystyle\inf_{\pi(\cdot):(2^{[N]}\times[N])^{n}\mapsto 2^{[N]}}\sup_{(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{uni}}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{D}\sim\otimes_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{v}}(\cdot|S_{k})}\big[\mathrm{SupOpt}_{\rho}(\pi(\mathbb{D});\bm{v},\bm{r})\big]

(B.123)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\inf_{\pi(\cdot):(2^{[N]}\times[N])^{n}\mapsto 2^{[N]}}\sup_{(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{uni}}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{D}\sim\otimes_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{v}}(\cdot|S_{k})}\big[\mathrm{SupOpt}_{\rho}(\widetilde{S}(\pi(\mathbb{D}));\bm{v},\bm{r})\big]

(B.124)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\inf_{\pi(\cdot):(2^{[N]}\times[N])^{n}\mapsto 2^{[N]}}\sup_{(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{uni}}}\bigg\{\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{18}\cdot\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(\widetilde{S}(\pi(\mathbb{D})))\cdot\Big(1-\exp\big(-r_{\max}/\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(\widetilde{S}(\pi(\mathbb{D})))\big)\Big)

(B.125)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{D}\sim\otimes_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{v}}(\cdot|S_{k})}\big[\Delta(S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}},\widetilde{S}(\pi(\mathbb{D})))\big]\bigg\}

(B.126)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{18}\cdot\inf_{(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{uni}}}\inf_{S\in[4K],|S|=K}\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S)\cdot\big(1-\exp(-r_{\max}/\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S))\big)

(B.127)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\inf_{\pi(\cdot):(2^{[N]}\times[N])^{n}\mapsto 2^{[N]}}\sup_{(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{uni}}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{D}\sim\otimes_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{v}}(\cdot|S_{k})}\big[\Delta(S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}},\pi(\mathbb{D}))\big]

(B.128)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\frac{1}{2304}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{C_{1}}{5}}\cdot\big(1-2^{-\underline{c}_{\rho}}\big)\cdot\frac{r_{\max}}{\log\left(\frac{2}{\frac{1}{2}\cdot c^{\dagger}_{\rho}\wedge\overline{c}_{\rho}\cdot\log 2}\right)}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{K}}{\sqrt{n_{\min}}}.

(B.129)

where the first inequality uses (
B.114
), the second inequality uses (
B.118
), the third inequality uses
\widetilde{S}(\pi(\mathbb{D}))\subseteq[4K]
with size
K
and the fact of (
B.105
), and the last inequality uses the upper and lower bounds (
B.89
) of the optimal dual variable
\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S)
for any assortment
S\subseteq[4K]
with size
|S|=K
and any instance
(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{uni}}
, together with Lemma
B.7
.
This completes the proof of Theorem
5.2
for the uniform revenue case.
∎

Appendix C
Proofs for Varying Robust Set Size Case

C.1
General Upper Bound for Suboptimality Gap

Theorem C.1

(Suboptimality of Algorithm
2
)
.

Conditioning on the observed assortments
\{S_{k}\}_{k=1}^{n}
, suppose that

\displaystyle n_{i}:=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbf{1}\{i\in S_{k}\}\geq 138\cdot\max\left\{1,\frac{256}{138v_{i}}\right\}\cdot(1+v_{\max})(1+Kv_{\max})\log(3N/\delta),\quad\forall i\in S^{\star},

(C.1)

then with probability at least
1-2\delta
, the robust expected revenue of the assortment
\widehat{S}
satisfies

\displaystyle R_{\rho(\cdot,\cdot)}(S^{\star};\bm{v})-R_{\rho(\cdot,\cdot)}(\widehat{S};\bm{v})

(C.2)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{3r_{\max}}{1+v(S^{\star})/2-(1-e^{-\rho_{0}})\cdot(1+v_{\mathrm{tot}})}

(C.3)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\sum_{i\in S^{\star}}\left(16\sqrt{\frac{v_{i}(1+v_{i})\log(3N/\delta)}{\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbf{1}\{i_{k}\in S_{k}\}\cdot(1+v(S_{k}))^{-1}}}+\frac{88(1+v_{i})\log(3N/\delta)}{\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbf{1}\{i_{k}\in S_{k}\}\cdot(1+v(S_{k}))^{-1}}\right).

(C.4)

Proof of Theorem
C.1
.

See Appendix
C.2
for a detailed proof.
∎

C.2
Proof of Theorem
C.1

Proof of Theorem
5.3
.

Consider the following upper bounds of the suboptimality, in terms of the robust expected revenue defined in Example
2.2
, of the learned assortment
\widehat{S}
,

\displaystyle R_{\rho(\cdot,\cdot)}(S^{\star};\bm{v})-R_{\rho(\cdot,\cdot)}(\widehat{S};\bm{v})

\displaystyle=\sup_{\lambda\geq 0}\left\{\widetilde{H}(S^{\star},\lambda;\bm{v})\right\}-\sup_{\lambda\geq 0}\left\{\widetilde{H}(\widehat{S},\lambda;\bm{v})\right\}

(C.5)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\lambda\geq 0}\left\{\widetilde{H}(S^{\star},\lambda;\bm{v})\right\}-\sup_{\lambda\geq 0}\left\{\widetilde{H}(\widehat{S},\lambda;\bm{v}^{\mathrm{LCB}})\right\}

(C.6)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\lambda\geq 0}\left\{\widetilde{H}(S^{\star},\lambda;\bm{v})\right\}-\sup_{\lambda\geq 0}\left\{\widetilde{H}(S^{\star},\lambda;\bm{v}^{\mathrm{LCB}})\right\},

(C.7)

where the first inequality is due to the dual representation (
4.7
), the first inequality is by Lemma
D.3
which shows the monotonicity property associated with
\widetilde{H}
, and the last inequality is from the optimality of
\widehat{S}
under the nominal parameter
\bm{v}^{\mathrm{LCB}}
.
Now we further upper bound the suboptimality gap via (
C.7
) as

\displaystyle R_{\rho(\cdot,\cdot)}(S^{\star};\bm{v})-R_{\rho(\cdot,\cdot)}(\widehat{S};\bm{v})

(C.8)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\sup_{0\leq\lambda\leq B(S^{\star};\bm{v})}\left\{-\lambda\cdot\log\left(\frac{\sum_{i\in S_{+}^{\star}}v_{i}\cdot\exp(-r_{i}/\lambda)}{\sum_{i\in S_{+}^{\star}}v_{i}}\right)-\lambda\cdot\rho_{0}+\lambda\cdot\log\left(e^{\rho_{0}}-\frac{a(\rho_{0})}{\sum_{i\in S_{+}^{\star}}v_{i}}\right)\right\}

(C.9)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\sup_{0\leq\lambda\leq B(S^{\star};\bm{v}^{\mathrm{LCB}})}\left\{-\lambda\!\cdot\!\log\!\left(\frac{\sum_{i\in S_{+}^{\star}}v_{i}^{\mathrm{LCB}}\cdot\exp(-r_{i}/\lambda)}{\sum_{i\in S_{+}^{\star}}v_{i}^{\mathrm{LCB}}}\right)-\lambda\!\cdot\!\rho_{0}+\lambda\!\cdot\!\log\left(e^{\rho_{0}}-\frac{a(\rho_{0})}{\sum_{i\in S_{+}^{\star}}v_{i}^{\mathrm{LCB}}}\right)\right\}

(C.10)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\underbrace{\sup_{\lambda\geq 0}\left\{\lambda\cdot\left(\log\left(\frac{\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}^{\mathrm{LCB}}\cdot\exp(-r_{i}/\lambda)}{\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}^{\mathrm{LCB}}}\cdot\right)-\log\left(\frac{\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}\cdot\exp(-r_{i}/\lambda)}{\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}}\right)\right)\right\}}_{\displaystyle{\text{Term (i)}}}

(C.11)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\underbrace{\sup_{0\leq\lambda\leq B(S^{\star};\bm{v}^{\mathrm{LCB}})}\left\{\lambda\cdot\left(\log\left(\frac{e^{\rho_{0}}\cdot\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}^{\mathrm{LCB}}}{e^{\rho_{0}}\cdot\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}^{\mathrm{LCB}}-a(\rho_{0})}\right)-\log\left(\frac{e^{\rho_{0}}\cdot\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}}{e^{\rho_{0}}\cdot\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}-a(\rho_{0})}\right)\right)\right\}}_{\displaystyle{\text{Term (ii)}}}.

(C.12)

where the first inequality applies (
C.7
) and the definition of function
\widetilde{H}
in (
4.6
), together with the definition that
a(\rho_{0}):=(e^{\rho_{0}}-1)(1+v_{\mathrm{tot}})
for the seek of simplicity.
The second inequality uses the inequality that
\sup_{x}f(x)-\sup_{x}g(x)\leq\sup_{x}\{f(x)-g(x)\}
.
Now we upper bound the two terms in (
C.8
) respectively.

For the first term in (
C.8
), it actually coincides with the right hand side of (
B.6
).
Therefore, by the same argument as proving Theorem
5.1
, we can obtain that with probability at least
1-2\delta
, it holds that

\displaystyle\text{Term (i)}\leq\frac{2\cdot r_{\max}}{\log 3}\cdot\frac{\sum_{i\in S_{+}^{\star}}v_{i}-v_{i}^{\mathrm{LCB}}}{\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}^{\mathrm{LCB}}}.

(C.13)

For the second term in (
C.8
), firstly consider that

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{e^{\rho_{0}}\cdot\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}^{\mathrm{LCB}}}{e^{\rho_{0}}\cdot\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}^{\mathrm{LCB}}-a(\rho_{0})}\right)-\log\left(\frac{e^{\rho_{0}}\cdot\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}}{e^{\rho_{0}}\cdot\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}-a(\rho_{0})}\right)

(C.14)

\displaystyle\qquad=\log\left(1+\frac{e^{\rho_{0}}\cdot\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}-a(\rho_{0})}{e^{\rho_{0}}\cdot\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}}\cdot\left(\frac{e^{\rho_{0}}\cdot\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}^{\mathrm{LCB}}}{e^{\rho_{0}}\cdot\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}^{\mathrm{LCB}}-a(\rho_{0})}-\frac{e^{\rho_{0}}\cdot\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}}{e^{\rho_{0}}\cdot\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}-a(\rho_{0})}\right)\right)

(C.15)

\displaystyle\qquad=\log\left(1+a(\rho_{0})\cdot\frac{e^{\rho_{0}}\cdot\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}-a(\rho_{0})}{e^{\rho_{0}}\cdot\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{e^{\rho_{0}}\cdot\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}^{\mathrm{LCB}}-a(\rho_{0})}-\frac{1}{e^{\rho_{0}}\cdot\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}-a(\rho_{0})}\right)\right)

(C.16)

\displaystyle\qquad=\log\left(1+\frac{a(\rho_{0})}{\left(\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}\right)\cdot\left(e^{\rho_{0}}\cdot\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}^{\mathrm{LCB}}-a(\rho_{0})\right)}\cdot\left(\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}-\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}^{\mathrm{LCB}}\right)\right)

(C.17)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{a(\rho_{0})}{\left(\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}\right)\cdot\left(e^{\rho_{0}}\cdot\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}^{\mathrm{LCB}}-a(\rho_{0})\right)}\cdot\left(\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}-\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}^{\mathrm{LCB}}\right),

(C.18)

where the last inequality uses that
\log(1+x)\leq x
for
x>-1
.
Meanwhile, the dual variable
\lambda
satisfies that

\displaystyle\lambda

\displaystyle\leq\frac{r_{\max}}{\log\left(\frac{e^{\rho_{0}}\cdot\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}^{\mathrm{LCB}}}{e^{\rho_{0}}\cdot\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}^{\mathrm{LCB}}-a(\rho_{0})}\right)}=\frac{r_{\max}}{\log\left(1+\frac{a(\rho_{0})}{e^{\rho_{0}}\cdot\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}^{\mathrm{LCB}}-a(\rho_{0})}\right)}\leq r_{\max}\cdot\frac{e^{\rho_{0}}\cdot\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}}{a(\rho_{0})},

(C.19)

where the last inequality uses that
\log(1+x)\geq x/(x+1)
for
x>-1
and Lemma
D.5
.
Consequently, we can upper bound the second term in (
C.8
) as

\displaystyle\text{Term (ii)}\leq r_{\max}\cdot\frac{\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}-\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}^{\mathrm{LCB}}}{\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}^{\mathrm{LCB}}-e^{-\rho_{0}}\cdot a(\rho_{0})}.

(C.20)

Therefore, by combining (
C.13
) and (
C.20
), we can obtain that with probability at least
1-2\delta
,

\displaystyle R_{\rho(\cdot,\cdot)}(S^{\star};\bm{v})-R_{\rho(\cdot,\cdot)}(\widehat{S};\bm{v})\leq 3r_{\max}\cdot\frac{\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}-\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}^{\mathrm{LCB}}}{\sum_{i\in S^{\star}_{+}}v_{i}^{\mathrm{LCB}}-(1-e^{-\rho_{0}})\cdot(1+v_{\mathrm{tot}})}.

(C.21)

Finally, by using Lemma
D.6
to control the error of the estimation
\bm{v}^{\mathrm{LCB}}
, we can obtain that

\displaystyle R_{\rho(\cdot,\cdot)}(S^{\star};\bm{v})-R_{\rho(\cdot,\cdot)}(\widehat{S};\bm{v})

(C.22)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{3r_{\max}}{1+v(S^{\star})/2-(1-e^{-\rho_{0}})\cdot(1+v_{\mathrm{tot}})}

(C.23)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\sum_{i\in S^{\star}}\left(16\sqrt{\frac{v_{i}(1+v_{i})\log(3N/\delta)}{\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbf{1}\{i_{k}\in S_{k}\}\cdot(1+v(S_{k}))^{-1}}}+\frac{88(1+v_{i})\log(3N/\delta)}{\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbf{1}\{i_{k}\in S_{k}\}\cdot(1+v(S_{k}))^{-1}}\right).

(C.24)

This completes the proof of Theorem
5.3
.
∎

C.3
Proof of Theorem
5.3

With the general results in Theorem
C.1
, we are ready to specify it to Theorem
5.3
.

Proof of Theorem
5.3
.

We prove the non-uniform revenue case and the uniform revenue case respectively as following.
It suffices to calculate the upper bounds for

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+v(S^{\star})/2-(1-e^{-\rho_{0}})\cdot(1+v_{\mathrm{tot}})}\cdot\sum_{i\in S^{\star}}\sqrt{\frac{v_{i}(1+v_{i})\log(3N/\delta)}{\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbf{1}\{i_{k}\in S_{k}\}\cdot(1+v(S_{k}))^{-1}}},

(C.25)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+v(S^{\star})/2-(1-e^{-\rho_{0}})\cdot(1+v_{\mathrm{tot}})}\cdot\sum_{i\in S^{\star}}\frac{(1+v_{i})\log(3N/\delta)}{\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbf{1}\{i_{k}\in S_{k}\}\cdot(1+v(S_{k}))^{-1}},

(C.26)

respectively, which results in different upper bounds under different setups.

Non-uniform revenue case.

For the first term in Theorem (
C.26
), we have the following upper bound,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+v(S^{\star})/2-(1-e^{-\rho_{0}})\cdot(1+v_{\mathrm{tot}})}\cdot\sum_{i\in S^{\star}}\sqrt{\frac{v_{i}(1+v_{i})\log(3N/\delta)}{\sum_{k=1}^{n}\bm{1}\{i\in S_{k}\}(1+\sum_{j\in S_{k}}v_{j})^{-1}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{1}{1+v(S^{\star})/2-(1-e^{-\rho_{0}})\cdot(1+v_{\mathrm{tot}})}\cdot\sqrt{(1+v_{\max})(1+Kv_{\max})\log(3N/\delta)}\cdot\sum_{i\in S^{\star}}\sqrt{\frac{v_{i}}{n_{i}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\sqrt{\frac{2(1+v_{\max})(1+Kv_{\max})\log(3N/\delta)}{1+v(S^{\star})/2-(1-e^{-\rho_{0}})\cdot(1+v_{\mathrm{tot}})}}\cdot\sqrt{\sum_{i\in S^{\star}}\frac{1}{n_{i}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{(1+v_{\max})K}{\sqrt{1+v(S^{\star})/2-(1-e^{-\rho_{0}})\cdot(1+v_{\mathrm{tot}})}}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{2\log(3N/\delta)}{\min_{i\in S^{\star}}n_{i}}},

where the first inequality uses that
v_{i}\leq v_{\max}
and the fact that

\displaystyle 1+\sum_{j\in S_{k}}v_{j}\leq 1+Kv_{\max},\quad\forall k\in[n],

(C.27)

the second inequality is based on Cauchy-Schwartz inequality that

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in S^{\star}}\sqrt{\frac{v_{i}}{n_{i}}}\leq\sqrt{\sum_{i\in S^{\star}}v_{i}}\cdot\sqrt{\sum_{i\in S^{\star}}\frac{1}{n_{i}}}\leq\sqrt{2}\cdot\sqrt{1+\sum_{i\in S^{\star}}v_{i}-(1-e^{-\rho_{0}})\cdot(1+v_{\mathrm{tot}})}\cdot\sqrt{\sum_{i\in S^{\star}}\frac{1}{n_{i}}},

(C.28)

and the last inequality uses the fact that
1+Kv_{\max}\leq K(1+v_{\max})
and the inequality that

\displaystyle\sqrt{\sum_{i\in S^{\star}}\frac{1}{n_{i}}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{K}{\min_{i\in S^{\star}}n_{i}}}.

(C.29)

For the second term in (
B.39
), we similarly have the following upper bound,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+v(S^{\star})/2-(1-e^{-\rho_{0}})\cdot(1+v_{\mathrm{tot}})}\cdot\sum_{i\in S^{\star}}\frac{(1+v_{i})\log(3N/\delta)}{\sum_{k=1}^{n}\bm{1}\{i\in S_{k}\}(1+\sum_{j\in S_{k}}v_{j})^{-1}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{1}{1+v(S^{\star})/2-(1-e^{-\rho_{0}})\cdot(1+v_{\mathrm{tot}})}\cdot\frac{(1+v_{\max})^{2}K^{2}\log(3N/\delta)}{\min_{i\in S^{\star}}n_{i}}.

This finishes the proof of Theorem
5.3
for the non-uniform revenue case.

Uniform revenue case.

In the uniform revenue setting, due to Proposition
3.2
, we always have that

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in S^{\star}}v_{i}\geq\sum_{i\in S}v_{i},\quad\text{for any assortment $S$ with $|S|\leq K$}.

(C.30)

Consequently, the first term in (
C.26
) can be upper bounded as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+v(S^{\star})/2-(1-e^{-\rho_{0}})\cdot(1+v_{\mathrm{tot}})}\cdot\sum_{i\in S^{\star}}\sqrt{\frac{v_{i}(1+v_{i})\log(3N/\delta)}{\sum_{k=1}^{n}\bm{1}\{i\in S_{k}\}(1+\sum_{j\in S_{k}}v_{j})^{-1}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{\sqrt{1+\sum_{j\in S^{\star}}v_{j}}\cdot\sqrt{(1+v_{\max})\log(3N/\delta)}}{1+\sum_{j\in S^{\star}}v_{j}/2-(1-e^{-\rho_{0}})\cdot(1+v_{\mathrm{tot}})}\cdot\sum_{i\in S^{\star}}\sqrt{\frac{v_{i}}{n_{i}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{\sqrt{1+\sum_{j\in S^{\star}}v_{j}}}{\sqrt{1+\sum_{j\in S^{\star}}v_{j}/2-(1-e^{-\rho_{0}})\cdot(1+v_{\mathrm{tot}})}}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{2(1+v_{\max})K\log(3N/\delta)}{\min_{i\in S^{\star}}n_{i}}},

where the first inequality uses (
C.30
) and
v_{i}\leq v_{\max}
, the second inequality uses Cauchy-Schwartz inequality that

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in S^{\star}}\sqrt{\frac{v_{i}}{n_{i}}}\leq\sqrt{\sum_{i\in S^{\star}}v_{i}}\cdot\sqrt{\sum_{i\in S^{\star}}\frac{1}{n_{i}}}\leq\sqrt{2}\cdot\sqrt{1+\sum_{i\in S^{\star}}v_{i}-(1-e^{-\rho_{0}})\cdot(1+v_{\mathrm{tot}})}\cdot\sqrt{\sum_{i\in S^{\star}}\frac{1}{n_{i}}},

(C.31)

together with the fact that

\displaystyle\sqrt{\sum_{i\in S^{\star}}\frac{1}{n_{i}}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{K}{\min_{i\in S^{\star}}n_{i}}}.

(C.32)

Similarly, for the second term in (
B.39
), we have the following upper bound that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+\sum_{j\in S^{\star}}v_{j}/2-(1-e^{-\rho_{0}})\cdot(1+v_{\mathrm{tot}})}\cdot\sum_{i\in S^{\star}}\frac{(1+v_{i})\log(3N/\delta)}{\sum_{k=1}^{n}\bm{1}\{i\in S_{k}\}(1+\sum_{j\in S_{k}}v_{j})^{-1}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{1+\sum_{j\in S^{\star}}v_{j}}{1+\sum_{j\in S^{\star}}v_{j}/2-(1-e^{-\rho_{0}})\cdot(1+v_{\mathrm{tot}})}\cdot\frac{(1+v_{\max})K\log(3N/\delta)}{\min_{i\in S^{\star}}n_{i}},

as desired, finishing the proof of Theorem
5.3
for the uniform revenue case.
∎

C.4
Proof of Theorem
5.4

In this section, we prove the suboptimality lower bounds for Example
2.2
. We first prove the lower bound for
the general non-uniform revenue case, after which we prove the lower bound for the uniform revenue case.

Proof of Theorem
5.4
: non-uniform revenue case
.

The overall structure of the proof of Theorem
5.4
resembles that of Theorem
5.2
, but involves some key technical differences in the details.

Step 1: construction of hard instance class and offline dataset.

The construction of both the hard instances and the offline data for the non-uniform revenue case coincide with those of Theorem
5.2
for the non-uniform revenue case.
We refer readers to Section
B.4
for the detailed introduction and definitions.
For clarity, we let the hard instance class
\mathcal{V}
be defined in the same way as in (
B.50
), that is,

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}:=\Big\{\text{$(\bm{v},\bm{r})$ induced by $(\mathcal{N}^{\star},\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}},\mathcal{N}^{0})$ via \eqref{eq:_proof_lower_bound_0}}\,\Big|\,\text{$\mathcal{N}^{\star}\in\mathcal{F}$ with $\mathcal{F}$ given by Lemma~\ref{lem-packing-number}}\Big\}.

(C.33)

The following shows that the optimal robust assortment induced by a division
(\mathcal{N}^{\star},\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}},\mathcal{N}^{0})
is exactly
\mathcal{N}^{\star}
.

Lemma C.2

(Optimal robust assortment for class (
C.33
))
.

For any problem instance
(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}
associated with the division
(\mathcal{N}^{\star},\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}},\mathcal{N}^{0})
, its optimal robust assortment
S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}
in the sense of Example
2.2
is
S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}=\mathcal{N}^{\star}
.

Proof of Lemma
C.2
.

By definition, for any instance
(\bm{v},\bm{r})
with partitions given by
(\mathcal{N}^{\star},\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}},\mathcal{N}^{0})
, the robust expected revenue of any assortment
S
with
|S|\leq K
is given by

\displaystyle R_{\rho(\cdot;\cdot)}(S;\bm{v},\bm{r})

\displaystyle=\sup_{\lambda\geq 0}\left\{-\lambda\cdot\log\left(\frac{1+|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|+e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}\cdot(1/K\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}}|+(1/K+\epsilon)\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|)}{1+|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|+1/K\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}}|+(1/K+\epsilon)\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}\right)\right\}

(C.34)

\displaystyle=\sup_{\lambda\geq 0}\left\{-\lambda\cdot\log\left(e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}+\frac{(1+|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|)\cdot(1-e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}})+a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})\cdot e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}}{1+|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|+1/K\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}}|+(1/K+\epsilon)\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}\right)\right\}.

(C.35)

Obviously, the right hand side of the above equality is optimized (over
S
) when
S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}}=\emptyset
and
|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|=K-|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|
.
Therefore, it suffices to consider how to maximize

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\phi(|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|):=\sup_{\lambda\geq 0}\left\{-\lambda\cdot\log\left(e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}+\frac{(1+|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|)\cdot(1-e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}})+a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})\cdot e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}}{1+|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|+(1/K+\epsilon)\cdot(K-|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|)-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}\right)\right\},

(C.36)

over
|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|\leq K
.
To this end, consider that the term inside the logarithm in (
C.36
) can be analyzed by

\displaystyle\frac{(1+|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|)\cdot(1-e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}})+a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})\cdot e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}}{1+|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|+(1/K+\epsilon)\cdot(K-|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|)-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}

(C.37)

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1-e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}}{1-1/K-\epsilon}+\frac{1-(1-a)\cdot e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}-(2+K\epsilon-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0}))\cdot\frac{1-e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}}{1-1/K-\epsilon}}{(1-1/K-\epsilon)\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|+2+K\epsilon-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}.

(C.38)

We find that the sign of the numerator of the second term on the right hand side of (
C.38
) is given by

\displaystyle 1-(1-a)\cdot e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}-\big(2+K\epsilon-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})\big)\cdot\frac{1-e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}}{1-1/K-\epsilon}\leq 0\,\,\,\Leftrightarrow\,\,\,\lambda\leq\lambda_{0},

(C.39)

where the threshold
\lambda_{0}
is given by

\lambda_{0}:=r_{\max}/\log(((1+1/K-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0}))(1+K\epsilon))/((1+1/K)(1+K\epsilon)-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})))
.
Thus we can further calculate the optimal value of (
C.36
) via

\displaystyle\sup_{|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|\leq K}\phi(|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|)

\displaystyle=\sup_{|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|\leq K}\max\Bigg\{\sup_{0\leq\lambda\leq\lambda_{0}}\left\{\psi(|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|;\lambda)\right\},\sup_{\lambda>\lambda_{0}}\left\{\psi(|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|;\lambda)\right\}\Bigg\}

(C.40)

\displaystyle=\max\Bigg\{\sup_{|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|\leq K}\sup_{0\leq\lambda\leq\lambda_{0}}\left\{\psi(|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|;\lambda)\right\},\sup_{|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|\leq K}\sup_{\lambda>\lambda_{0}}\left\{\psi(|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|;\lambda)\right\}\Bigg\},

(C.41)

where for notational simplicity we denote

\displaystyle\psi(|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|;\lambda):=-\lambda\cdot\log\left(e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}+\frac{(1+|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|)\cdot(1-e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}})+a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})\cdot e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}}{1+|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|+(1/K+\epsilon)\cdot(K-|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|)-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}\right).

(C.42)

Notice that for
\lambda>\lambda_{0}
, according to (
C.39
), we have that

\displaystyle\sup_{|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|\leq K}\sup_{\lambda>\lambda_{0}}\psi(|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|;\lambda)=\sup_{\lambda>\lambda_{0}}\psi(K;\lambda)\leq\sup_{\lambda\geq 0}\psi(K;\lambda)=R_{\rho(\cdot;\cdot)}(\mathcal{N}^{0}_{K};\bm{v},\bm{r})=0,

(C.43)

where the second equality uses the dual representation of the robust expected revenue of an assortment
\mathcal{N}^{0}_{K}
which denotes any assortment being a subset of
\mathcal{N}^{0}
and of size
K
, and the last equality uses the fact the any assortment containing items solely form
\mathcal{N}^{0}
has robust expected revenue
0
.
Meanwhile, for
\lambda\leq\lambda_{0}
, according to (
C.39
), we have the following,

\displaystyle\sup_{|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|\leq K}\sup_{0\leq\lambda\leq\lambda_{0}}\psi(|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|;\lambda)=\sup_{0\leq\lambda\leq\lambda_{0}}\psi(0;\lambda).

(C.44)

Moreover, for
0\leq\lambda<\lambda_{+}:=r_{\max}/\log((1+K\epsilon)/(1+K\epsilon-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0}))
, it holds that
\psi(0;\lambda)>0
.
Therefore, we can conclude that

\displaystyle\sup_{S\subseteq[N],|S|\leq K}R_{\rho(\cdot;\cdot)}(S;\bm{v},\bm{r})=\sup_{|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|\leq K}\phi(|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|)=\sup_{0\leq\lambda<\lambda_{+}\wedge\lambda_{0}}\psi(0;\lambda),

(C.45)

and the optimal value is achieved when the assortment
S
satisfies
S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}=\emptyset
and
S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}}=\emptyset
, i.e.,
S=\mathcal{N}^{\star}
.
This completes the proof of Lemma
D.4
.
∎

Finally, we take the same construction of the observational data
\mathbb{D}
as in (
B.55
), that is,
\mathbb{D}=\{(S_{k},i_{k})\}_{k=1}^{n}
.
and each assortment
S_{k}
is given by

\displaystyle S_{k}:=\left\{\left\lceil\frac{k}{n_{\min}}\right\rceil,4K+2,\cdots,5K\right\}.

(C.46)

Step 2: establish the minimax lower bound.

For any
(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}
and
any assortment
S\subseteq[N]
with
|S|\leq K
, we need to lower bound the suboptimality of
S
under the instance
(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}
.
In the following, we show that it suffices to consider assortments
S\subseteq\mathcal{N}^{\star}\cup\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}}=[4K]
with
|S|=K
.
This approach has been used when we prove Theorem
5.2
, but here we need more delicate proofs to showcase the correctness of the argument, which we conclude in the following lemma.

Lemma C.3
.

Under the same setups in this section, for any assortment
S\subseteq[N]
with
|S|\leq K
, and for any instance
(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}
, it holds that

\displaystyle R_{\rho(\cdot;\cdot)}(S;\bm{v},\bm{r})\leq R_{\rho(\cdot;\cdot)}(\widetilde{S}(S);\bm{v},\bm{r}),\quad\text{where}\quad\widetilde{S}(S):=(S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star})\cup\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}}_{K-|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|}\subseteq\mathcal{N}^{\star}\cup\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}}=[4K],

(C.47)

where
\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}}_{K-|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|}
is any
|K-|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|
-sized subset of
\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}}
.

Proof of Lemma
C.3
.

We first prove that

\displaystyle R_{\rho(\cdot;\cdot)}(S;\bm{v},\bm{r})\leq R_{\rho(\cdot;\cdot)}(S^{\prime}(S);\bm{v},\bm{r}),\quad\text{where}\quad S^{\prime}(S):=(S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star})\cup(S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0})\cap\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}}_{K-|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|-|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|}.

(C.48)

This is because, by the dual representation,

\displaystyle R_{\rho(\cdot;\cdot)}(S;\bm{v},\bm{r})

(C.49)

\displaystyle\qquad=\sup_{\lambda\geq 0}\left\{-\lambda\cdot\log\left(e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}+\frac{(1+|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|)\cdot(1-e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}})+a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})\cdot e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}}{1+|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|+1/K\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}}|+(1/K+\epsilon)\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}\right)\right\},

(C.50)

and by the fact that
|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|+|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}}|+|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|\leq K
,

\displaystyle\frac{(1+|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|)\cdot(1-e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}})+a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})\cdot e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}}{1+|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|+1/K\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}}|+(1/K+\epsilon)\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}

(C.51)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\frac{(1+|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|)\cdot(1-e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}})+a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})\cdot e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}}{1+|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|+1/K\cdot(K-|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|-|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|)+(1/K+\epsilon)\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}.

(C.52)

Thus
R_{\rho(\cdot;\cdot)}(S;\bm{v},\bm{r})\leq R_{\rho(\cdot;\cdot)}(S^{\prime}(S);\bm{v},\bm{r})
.
Now we further prove that
R_{\rho(\cdot;\cdot)}(S^{\prime}(S);\bm{v},\bm{r})\leq R_{\rho(\cdot;\cdot)}(\widetilde{S}(S);\bm{v},\bm{r})
.
To simplify the notations, let’s now directly assume that
|S|=K
and prove
R_{\rho(\cdot;\cdot)}(S;\bm{v},\bm{r})\leq R_{\rho(\cdot;\cdot)}(\widetilde{S}(S);\bm{v},\bm{r})
.
To this end, by calculation, we have that

\displaystyle R_{\rho(\cdot;\cdot)}(S;\bm{v},\bm{r})=\sup_{\lambda\geq 0}\left\{-\lambda\cdot\log\left(\frac{1+e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}\cdot(1+\epsilon\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|)+(1-1/K\cdot e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}})\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|}{2+\epsilon\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|+(1-1/K)\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}\right)\right\},

(C.53)

where we have applied
|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|+|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}}|+|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|=K
.
Meanwhile,

\displaystyle R_{\rho(\cdot;\cdot)}(\widetilde{S}(S);\bm{v},\bm{r})=\sup_{\lambda\geq 0}\left\{-\lambda\cdot\log\left(\frac{1+e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}\cdot(1+\epsilon\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|)}{2+\epsilon\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}\right)\right\}.

(C.54)

To prove that
R_{\rho(\cdot;\cdot)}(S;\bm{v},\bm{r})\leq R_{\rho(\cdot;\cdot)}(\widetilde{S}(S);\bm{v},\bm{r})
, consider the following,

\displaystyle R_{\rho(\cdot;\cdot)}(S;\bm{v},\bm{r})

\displaystyle=\sup_{\lambda\geq 0}\left\{-\lambda\cdot\log\left(\frac{1+e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}\cdot(1+\epsilon\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|)+(1-1/K\cdot e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}})\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|}{2+\epsilon\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|+(1-1/K)\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}\right)\right\}

(C.55)

\displaystyle=-\lambda^{\star}_{S}\cdot\log\left(\frac{1+e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda^{\star}}}\cdot(1+\epsilon\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|)+(1-1/K\cdot e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda^{\star}_{S}}})\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|}{2+\epsilon\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})+(1-1/K)\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|}\right)

(C.56)

\displaystyle\leq-\lambda^{\star}_{S}\cdot\log\left(\frac{1+e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda^{\star}}}\cdot(1+\epsilon\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|)}{2+\epsilon\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}\right)

(C.57)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\lambda\geq 0}\left\{-\lambda\cdot\log\left(\frac{1+e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}\cdot(1+\epsilon\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|)}{2+\epsilon\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}\right)\right\}=R_{\rho(\cdot;\cdot)}(\widetilde{S}(S);\bm{v},\bm{r}),

(C.58)

where the first inequality uses the inequality that

\displaystyle\frac{a+x}{b+y}\geq\frac{a}{b}\quad\mathrm{if}\quad\text{(i)}\,x\geq y\geq 0\,\,\mathrm{and}\,\,\text{(ii)}\,b\geq a\geq 0.

(C.59)

To satisfy the conditions of the above inequality, notice that (i) is trivial since

\displaystyle x=(1-1/K\cdot e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda^{\star}_{S}}})\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|\geq(1-1/K)\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|=y.

(C.60)

To show the condition (ii), notice that by the optimality of
\lambda^{\star}_{S}
, we know that

\displaystyle\frac{a+x}{b+y}=\frac{1+e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda^{\star}}}\cdot(1+\epsilon\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|)+(1-1/K\cdot e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda^{\star}_{S}}})\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|}{2+\epsilon\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})+(1-1/K)\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|}\leq 1,

(C.61)

because we always have that
R_{\rho(\cdot;\cdot)}(S;\bm{v},\bm{r})\geq 0
.
This in turn gives that

\displaystyle a=a+x-x\leq b+y-x\leq b,

(C.62)

where the second inequality uses
y\leq x
.
Thus both two conditions are satisfied, completing the proof.
∎

Now for any assortment
S\subseteq\mathcal{N}^{\star}\cup\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}}=[4K]
with
|S|=K
(with abbreviation
S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}
as
S^{\star}
if no confusion),

\displaystyle\mathrm{SubOpt}_{\rho(\cdot;\cdot)}(S;\bm{v},\bm{r})=R_{\rho(\cdot;\cdot)}(S^{\star};\bm{v},\bm{r})-R_{\rho(\cdot;\cdot)}(S;\bm{v},\bm{r})

(C.63)

\displaystyle\qquad=\sup_{\lambda\geq 0}\left\{-\lambda\cdot\log\left(\frac{1+\exp(-r_{\max}/\lambda)\cdot(1+K\epsilon)}{2+K\epsilon-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}\right)\right\}

(C.64)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\sup_{\lambda\geq 0}\left\{-\lambda\cdot\log\left(\frac{1+\exp(-r_{\max}/\lambda)\cdot(1+|S\cap S^{\star}|\cdot\epsilon)}{2+|S\cap S^{\star}|\cdot\epsilon-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}\right)\right\}

(C.65)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq-\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S)\cdot\log\left(\frac{1+\exp(-r_{\max}/\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S))\cdot(1+K\epsilon)}{2+K\epsilon-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}\cdot\frac{2+|S\cap S^{\star}|\cdot\epsilon-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}{1+\exp(-r_{\max}/\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S))\cdot(1+|S\cap S^{\star}|\cdot\epsilon)}\right)

(C.66)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq-\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S)\cdot\left(\frac{1+\exp(-r_{\max}/\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S))\cdot(1+K\epsilon)}{2+K\epsilon-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}\cdot\frac{2+|S\cap S^{\star}|\cdot\epsilon-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}{1+\exp(-r_{\max}/\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S))\cdot(1+|S\cap S^{\star}|\cdot\epsilon)}-1\right)

(C.67)

\displaystyle\qquad=\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S)\cdot\Big(1-(1-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0}))\cdot\exp(-r_{\max}/\lambda_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}^{\star}(S))\Big)

(C.68)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\frac{2+|S\cap S^{\star}|\cdot\epsilon-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}{1+\exp(-r_{\max}/\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S))\cdot(1+|S\cap S^{\star}|\cdot\epsilon)}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{2+|S\cap S^{\star}|\cdot\epsilon-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}-\frac{1}{2+K\epsilon-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}\right)

(C.69)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\frac{\epsilon}{4\cdot(2+K\epsilon-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0}))^{2}}\cdot\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S)\cdot\Big(1-(1-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0}))\cdot\exp(-r_{\max}/\lambda_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}^{\star}(S))\Big)\cdot\Delta_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S),

(C.70)

where the first equality uses the dual representation of the robust expected revenue (Proposition
2.1
), the first inequality utilizes the notion of
\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S)
defined as

\displaystyle\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S):=\mathop{\mathrm{argmax}}_{\lambda\geq 0}\left\{-\lambda\cdot\log\left(\frac{1+\exp(-r_{\max}/\lambda)\cdot(1+|S\cap S^{\star}|\cdot\epsilon)}{2+|S\cap S^{\star}|\cdot\epsilon-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}\right)\right\},

(C.71)

the second inequality
\log(1+x)\leq x
for
x>-1
, and the last inequality uses the fact that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2+|S\cap S^{\star}|\cdot\epsilon-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}-\frac{1}{2+K\epsilon-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2\cdot(2+K\epsilon-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0}))^{2}}\cdot\underbrace{\Delta(S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}},S)}_{\displaystyle{:=\Delta_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S)}},

(C.72)

and the fact that

\displaystyle\frac{2+|S\cap S^{\star}|\cdot\epsilon-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}{1+\exp(-r_{\max}/\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S))\cdot(1+|S\cap S^{\star}|\cdot\epsilon)}\geq\frac{1}{2}.

(C.73)

Step 2.1: upper and lower bound the dual variable.

Now we need to upper and lower bound the dual variable
\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S)
defined in (
C.71
). Thanks to Lemma
C.3
, we only need to consider
\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S)
for
(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}
and
S\subseteq[4K]
with
|S|=K
.
On the one hand, by Proposition
2.1
, it follows directly that

\displaystyle\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S)\leq\frac{r_{\max}}{\rho(\bm{v};S)}=\frac{r_{\max}}{\log\left(\frac{2+|S\cap S^{\star}|\cdot\epsilon}{2+|S\cap S^{\star}|\cdot\epsilon-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}\right)}\leq\frac{r_{\max}}{\log\left(\frac{2+K\cdot\epsilon}{2+K\cdot\epsilon-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}\right)}.

(C.74)

On the other hand, we now lower bound the dual variable as follows.
For simplicity, let’s define the dual representation function as following, given
(\bm{v},\bm{r};S)\in\mathcal{V}\times 2^{[4K]}
with
|S|=K
,

\displaystyle h_{\bm{v},\bm{r},S}(\lambda):=-\lambda\cdot\log\left(\frac{1+\exp(-r_{\max}/\lambda)\cdot(1+|S\cap S^{\star}|\cdot\epsilon)}{2+|S\cap S^{\star}|\cdot\epsilon-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}\right).

(C.75)

Similar to the proofs in Appendix
B.4
(
Step 2.1
), to prove
\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S)\geq\underline{\lambda}
for some
\underline{\lambda}>0
, it suffices to prove that
\partial_{\lambda}h_{\bm{v},\bm{r},S}(\underline{\lambda})\geq 0
.
To this end, we first lower bound the derivative of
h_{\bm{v},\bm{r},S}
with respect to
\lambda
as following,

\displaystyle\partial_{\lambda}h_{\bm{v},\bm{r},S}(\lambda)

\displaystyle=-\log\left(\frac{1+\exp(-r_{\max}/\lambda)\cdot(1+|S\cap S^{\star}|\cdot\epsilon)}{2+|S\cap S^{\star}|\cdot\epsilon-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}\right)

(C.76)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}\cdot\frac{\exp(-r_{\max}/\lambda)\cdot(1+|S\cap S^{\star}|\cdot\epsilon)}{1+\exp(-r_{\max}/\lambda)\cdot(1+|S\cap S^{\star}|\cdot\epsilon)}

(C.77)

\displaystyle=\log\left(2+|S\cap S^{\star}|\cdot\epsilon-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})\right)-\log\Big(1+\exp(-r_{\max}/\lambda)\cdot(1+|S\cap S^{\star}|\cdot\epsilon)\Big)

(C.78)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}\cdot\frac{\exp(-r_{\max}/\lambda)\cdot(1+|S\cap S^{\star}|\cdot\epsilon)}{1+\exp(-r_{\max}/\lambda)\cdot(1+|S\cap S^{\star}|\cdot\epsilon)}.

(C.79)

Now consider the same reparametrization as (
B.80
), we can further handle the right hand side of (
C.79
) as

\displaystyle\partial_{\lambda}h_{\bm{v},\bm{r},S}(\lambda)

\displaystyle=\log\left(1-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})+x\right)-\log(1+u)+\log\left(\frac{u}{x}\right)\cdot\frac{u}{1+u}

(C.80)

\displaystyle\geq\log\left(1-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})+x\right)-\log(1+u)+\log\left(\frac{u}{2}\right)\cdot u.

(C.81)

Taking
u=(c^{\clubsuit}_{\rho}\wedge 1/2)\cdot\log(1-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})+x)\leq 2
, where
c^{\clubsuit}_{\rho}
is given by solving the equation that

\displaystyle c^{\clubsuit}_{\rho}:=\mathop{\mathrm{argmax}}_{c\geq 0}\left\{c\cdot\log\left(\frac{(c\wedge 1/2)\cdot\log(1+\overline{c}_{\rho})}{2}\right)\geq-\frac{1}{2}\right\},

(C.82)

It holds that
c^{\clubsuit}_{\rho}
is an absolute positive constant which only dependents on the constant
\overline{c}_{\rho}
.
We then lower bound the right hand side of (
C.81
) by

\displaystyle\partial_{\lambda}h_{\bm{v},\bm{r},S}(\lambda)

\displaystyle\geq\log\left(1-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})+x\right)-\log\left(1+\frac{1}{2}\cdot\log(1-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})+x)\right)

(C.83)

\displaystyle\qquad+c^{\clubsuit}_{\rho}\cdot\log\left(\frac{(c^{\clubsuit}_{\rho}\wedge 1/2)\cdot\log(1-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})+x)}{2}\right)\cdot\log\left(1-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})+x\right)

(C.84)

\displaystyle\geq\log\left(1-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})+x\right)-\frac{1}{2}\cdot\log\left(1-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})+x\right)

(C.85)

\displaystyle\qquad+c^{\clubsuit}_{\rho}\cdot\log\left(\frac{(c^{\clubsuit}_{\rho}\wedge 1/2)\cdot\log(1+\overline{c}_{\rho})}{2}\right)\cdot\log\left(1-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})+x\right)

(C.86)

\displaystyle\geq\log\left(1-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})+x\right)-\frac{1}{2}\cdot\log\left(1-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})+x\right)-\frac{1}{2}\cdot\log\left(1-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})+x\right)=0,

(C.87)

where the first inequality uses the choice of
u
, the second inequality uses the inequality that
\log(1+x)\geq x
for
x>-1
, and the fact that
x\geq 1
, and that
1-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})\geq\overline{c}_{\rho}
under the condition on
\rho_{0}
that

\displaystyle\rho_{0}\leq\log\left(\frac{1+v_{\mathrm{tot}}(\bm{v})}{\overline{c}_{\rho}+v_{\mathrm{tot}}(\bm{v})}\right)=\log\left(\frac{5+K\epsilon+N-4K}{\overline{c}_{\rho}+4+K\epsilon+N-4K}\right).

(C.88)

The last inequality uses property (
C.82
) that
c^{\clubsuit}_{\rho}
satisfies.
Therefore, with the above choice of
u
, the associated
\lambda
satisfies
\partial h_{\bm{v},\bm{r},S}(\lambda)\geq 0
.
Thus, using the reparametrization formula (
B.80
), we get back to the desired
\underline{\lambda}
as

\displaystyle\underline{\lambda}:=\frac{r_{\max}}{\log\left(\frac{x}{u}\right)}=\frac{r_{\max}}{\log\left(\frac{x}{(c^{\clubsuit}_{\rho}\wedge 1/2)\cdot\log(1-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})+x)}\right)}.

(C.89)

Consequently, we can conclude that the optimal dual variable is lower bounded by

\displaystyle\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S)\geq\underline{\lambda}=\frac{r_{\max}}{\log\left(\frac{x}{(c^{\clubsuit}_{\rho}\wedge 1/2)\cdot\log(1-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})+x)}\right)}\geq\frac{r_{\max}}{\log\left(\frac{2}{(c^{\clubsuit}_{\rho}\wedge 1/2)\cdot\log(1+\overline{c}_{\rho})}\right)}.

(C.90)

Step 2.2: lower bound the minimax rate of the quantity
\Delta(S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}},\pi(\mathbb{D}))
.

This coincides with the
Step 2.2
in the proof for constant robust set size case.
See Lemma
B.4
in Appendix
B.4
.

Step 2.3: finishing the proof.

We can lower bound the expected suboptimality gap by

\displaystyle\inf_{\pi(\cdot):(2^{[N]}\times[N])^{n}\mapsto 2^{[N]}}\sup_{(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{D}\sim\otimes_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{v}}(\cdot|S_{k})}\big[\mathrm{SupOpt}_{\rho(\cdot;\cdot)}(\pi(\mathbb{D});\bm{v},\bm{r})\big]

(C.91)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\inf_{\pi(\cdot):(2^{[N]}\times[N])^{n}\mapsto 2^{[N]}}\sup_{(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{D}\sim\otimes_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{v}}(\cdot|S_{k})}\big[\mathrm{SupOpt}_{\rho(\cdot;\cdot)}(\widetilde{S}(\pi(\mathbb{D}));\bm{v},\bm{r})\big]

(C.92)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\inf_{\pi(\cdot):(2^{[N]}\times[N])^{n}\mapsto 2^{[N]}}\sup_{(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}}\bigg\{\frac{\epsilon}{4\cdot(2+K\epsilon-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0}))^{2}}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{D}\sim\otimes_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{v}}(\cdot|S_{k})}\bigg[\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(\widetilde{S}(\pi(\mathbb{D})))

(C.93)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\Big(1-(1-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0}))\cdot\exp\big(-r_{\max}/\lambda_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}^{\star}(\widetilde{S}(\pi(\mathbb{D})))\big)\Big)\cdot\Delta(S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}},\widetilde{S}(\pi(\mathbb{D})))\bigg]\bigg\}

(C.94)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\inf_{S\subseteq[4k],|S|=K}\sup_{(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}}\bigg\{\frac{\epsilon}{4\cdot(2+K\epsilon-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0}))^{2}}\cdot\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S)\cdot\Big(1-(1-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0}))\cdot\exp\big(-r_{\max}/\lambda_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}^{\star}(S)\big)\Big)\bigg\}

(C.95)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\inf_{\pi(\cdot):(2^{[N]}\times[N])^{n}\mapsto 2^{[N]}}\sup_{(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}}\bigg\{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{D}\sim\otimes_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{v}}(\cdot|S_{k})}\big[\Delta(S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}},\pi(\mathbb{D}))\big]\bigg\}

(C.96)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\inf_{S\subseteq[4k],|S|=K}\sup_{(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}}\bigg\{\frac{\epsilon}{4\cdot(2+K\epsilon-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0}))^{2}}\cdot\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S)\cdot\Big(1-(1-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0}))\cdot\exp\big(-r_{\max}/\lambda_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}^{\star}(S)\big)\Big)\bigg\}

(C.97)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\frac{1}{128}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{C_{1}}{5}}\cdot\frac{K}{\epsilon}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{1}{\min_{i\in S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}}n_{i}}},

(C.98)

where the first inequality uses Lemma
C.3
to reduce any potential output
\pi(\mathbb{D})
into an assortment
S(\pi(\mathbb{D}))\subseteq[4K]
, the second inequality uses (
C.70
), the third inequality uses that
\widetilde{S}(\cdot)\subseteq[4K]
and the trivial fact that

\displaystyle\inf_{\pi(\cdot):(2^{[N]}\times[N])^{n}\mapsto 2^{[N]}}\sup_{(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}}\bigg\{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{D}\sim\otimes_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{v}}(\cdot|S_{k})}\big[\Delta(S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}},\widetilde{S}(\pi(\mathbb{D})))\big]\bigg\}

(C.99)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\inf_{\pi(\cdot):(2^{[N]}\times[N])^{n}\mapsto 2^{[N]}}\sup_{(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}}\bigg\{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{D}\sim\otimes_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{v}}(\cdot|S_{k})}\big[\Delta(S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}},\pi(\mathbb{D}))\big]\bigg\},

(C.100)

and the last inequality uses Lemma
B.5
and the fact that
n_{\min}=\min_{i\in S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}}n_{i}
.
To move on, using the upper and lower bounds on the dual variable, i.e., (
C.74
) and (
C.90
), we have that for any
S\subseteq[4K]
with
|S|=K
,

\displaystyle\frac{r_{\max}}{\log\left(\frac{2}{(c^{\clubsuit}_{\rho}\wedge 1/2)\cdot\log(1+\overline{c}_{\rho})}\right)}\leq\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S)\leq\frac{r_{\max}}{\log\left(\frac{2+K\cdot\epsilon}{2+K\cdot\epsilon-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}\right)},

(C.101)

and consequently that

\displaystyle 1-(1-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0}))\cdot\exp\big(-r_{\max}/\lambda_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}^{\star}(S)\big)

\displaystyle\geq 1-(1-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0}))\cdot\frac{2+K\epsilon-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}{2+K\epsilon}

(C.102)

\displaystyle\geq a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})\cdot\frac{2+K\epsilon-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}{2}

(C.103)

\displaystyle\geq\underline{c}_{\rho}\cdot\frac{2+K\epsilon-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}{2},

(C.104)

where the last inequality follows from that
a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})\geq\underline{c}_{\rho}
under the condition on the parameter
\rho_{0}
that

\displaystyle\rho_{0}\geq\log\left(\frac{1+v_{\mathrm{tot}}(\bm{v})}{1+v_{\mathrm{tot}}(\bm{v})-\underline{c}_{\rho}}\right)=\log\left(\frac{5+K\epsilon+N-4K}{5+K\epsilon+N-4K-\underline{c}_{\rho}}\right)

(C.105)

Therefore, we can conclude that the expected suboptimality is lower bounded by

\displaystyle\inf_{\pi(\cdot):(2^{[N]}\times[N])^{n}\mapsto 2^{[N]}}\sup_{(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{D}\sim\otimes_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{v}}(\cdot|S_{k})}\big[\mathrm{SupOpt}_{\rho(\cdot;\cdot)}(\pi(\mathbb{D});\bm{v},\bm{r})\big]

(C.106)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\frac{\underline{c}_{\rho}\cdot\sqrt{C_{1}}}{1024\sqrt{5}\cdot\log\left(\frac{2}{(c^{\clubsuit}_{\rho}\wedge 1/2)\cdot\log(1+\overline{c}_{\rho})}\right)}\cdot\frac{r_{\max}\cdot K}{2+K\epsilon-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{1}{\min_{i\in S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}}n_{i}}}

(C.107)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\frac{\underline{c}_{\rho}\cdot\sqrt{C_{1}}}{2048\sqrt{5}\cdot\log\left(\frac{2}{(c^{\clubsuit}_{\rho}\wedge 1/2)\cdot\log(1+\overline{c}_{\rho})}\right)}\cdot\frac{r_{\max}\cdot K}{1+(1+K\epsilon)/2-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{1}{\min_{i\in S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}}n_{i}}}

(C.108)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\frac{\underline{c}_{\rho}\cdot\sqrt{C_{1}}}{2048\sqrt{10}\cdot\log\left(\frac{2}{(c^{\clubsuit}_{\rho}\wedge 1/2)\cdot\log(1+\overline{c}_{\rho})}\right)}\cdot\frac{r_{\max}\cdot K}{\sqrt{1+(1+K\epsilon)/2-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{1}{\min_{i\in S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}}n_{i}}}

(C.109)

\displaystyle\qquad\cong\frac{r_{\max}\cdot K}{\sqrt{1+\sum_{j\in S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}}v_{j}/2-(1-e^{-\rho_{0}})\cdot(1+v_{\mathrm{tot}}(\bm{v}))}}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{1}{\min_{i\in S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}}n_{i}}},

(C.110)

where the first inequality combines (
C.98
), (
C.101
), and (
C.104
), and the last inequality hides global constants and uses the fact that
\sum_{j\in S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}}v_{j}=1+K\epsilon
.
This completes the proof of Theorem
5.4
for general non-uniform revenue case.
∎

Proof of Theorem
5.4
: uniform revenue case
.

The proof to the uniform revenue case resembles that for the general non-uniform revenue case, with the differences we highlight in the sequel.
Firstly, we construct the hard instance class
\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{uni}}
and the offline dataset
\mathbb{D}
following the same way as in the proof for Theorem
5.2
. We refer to Section
B.4
for details.
For the ease of readers, we repeat the definition of
\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{uni}}
here,

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{uni}}:=\Big\{\text{$(\bm{v},\bm{r})$ induced by $(\mathcal{N}^{\star},\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}},\mathcal{N}^{0})$ via \eqref{eq:_proof_lower_bound_2}}\,\Big|\,\text{$\mathcal{N}^{\star}\in\mathcal{F}$ with $\mathcal{F}$ given by Lemma~\ref{lem-packing-number}}\Big\}.

(C.111)

We have the following result for the optimal robust assortment associated with instances in the class
\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{uni}}
.

Lemma C.4

(Optimal robust assortment for class (
C.111
))
.

For any problem instance
(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{uni}}
associated
with the division
(\mathcal{N}^{\star},\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}},\mathcal{N}^{0})
, its optimal robust assortment
S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}
in the sense of Example
2.2
is
S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}=\mathcal{N}^{\star}
.

Proof of Lemma
C.4
.

By definition, for any instance
(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{uni}}
with partitions given by
(\mathcal{N}^{\star},\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}},\mathcal{N}^{0})
, the robust expected revenue of any assortment
S
is given by

\displaystyle R_{\rho(\cdot;\cdot)}(S;\bm{v},\bm{r})

(C.112)

\displaystyle\qquad=\sup_{\lambda\geq 0}\left\{-\lambda\cdot\log\left(\frac{1+e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}\cdot(1/K\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|+1/K\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}}|+(1/K+\epsilon^{\prime})\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|)}{1+1/K\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|+1/K\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}}|+(1/K+\epsilon^{\prime})\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}\right)\right\}

(C.113)

\displaystyle\qquad=\sup_{\lambda\geq 0}\left\{-\lambda\cdot\log\left(e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}+\frac{1-e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}+a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})\cdot e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}}{1+1/K\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|+1/K\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}}|+(1/K+\epsilon^{\prime})\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}\right)\right\}

(C.114)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\sup_{\lambda\geq 0}\left\{-\lambda\cdot\log\left(e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}+\frac{1-e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}+a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})\cdot e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}}{1+(1/K+\epsilon^{\prime})\cdot|\mathcal{N}^{\star}|-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}\right)\right\},

(C.115)

and the inequality becomes equality only when
S=\mathcal{N}^{\star}
, completing the proof of Lemma
B.6
.
∎

The rest of the proof for the uniform revenue case aligns well with the proofs for the general non-uniform case, with exceptional difference we discuss in detail in the sequel.

With the same spirit of Lemma
C.3
, we first demonstrate that it suffices to consider assortments
S\subseteq[4K]
satisfying
|S|=K
.
This is relatively easy to prove for the uniform revenue instances.
To see this, consider that for any assortment
S\subseteq[N]
with
|S|\leq K
, we have

\displaystyle R_{\rho(\cdot;\cdot)}(S;\bm{v},\bm{r})

(C.116)

\displaystyle\qquad=\sup_{\lambda\geq 0}\left\{-\lambda\cdot\log\left(e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}+\frac{1-e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}+a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})\cdot e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}}{1+1/K\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{0}|+1/K\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}}|+(1/K+\epsilon^{\prime})\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}\right)\right\}

(C.117)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\sup_{\lambda\geq 0}\left\{-\lambda\cdot\log\left(e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}+\frac{1-e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}+a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})\cdot e^{-\frac{r_{\max}}{\lambda}}}{1+1/K\cdot(K-|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|)+(1/K+\epsilon^{\prime})\cdot|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}\right)\right\}

(C.118)

\displaystyle\qquad=R_{\rho(\cdot;\cdot)}(\widetilde{S};\bm{v},\bm{r})\quad\text{where}\quad\widetilde{S}(S):=(S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star})\cup\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}}_{K-|S\cap\mathcal{N}^{\star}|}\subseteq\mathcal{N}^{\star}\cup\mathcal{N}^{\mathrm{c}}=[4K].

(C.119)

Therefore, now we only need to lower bound the suboptimality of any assortment
S\subseteq[4K]
with
|S|=K
.
To this end, we have the following,

\displaystyle\mathrm{SubOpt}_{\rho(\cdot;\cdot)}(S;\bm{v},\bm{r})=R_{\rho(\cdot;\cdot)}(S^{\star};\bm{v},\bm{r})-R_{\rho(\cdot;\cdot)}(S;\bm{v},\bm{r})

(C.120)

\displaystyle\qquad=\sup_{\lambda\geq 0}\left\{-\lambda\cdot\log\left(\frac{1+\exp(-r_{\max}/\lambda)\cdot(1+K\epsilon^{\prime})}{2+K\epsilon-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}\right)\right\}

(C.121)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\sup_{\lambda\geq 0}\left\{-\lambda\cdot\log\left(\frac{1+\exp(-r_{\max}/\lambda)\cdot(1+|S\cap S^{\star}|\cdot\epsilon^{\prime})}{2+|S\cap S^{\star}|\cdot\epsilon^{\prime}-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}\right)\right\}

(C.122)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{4\cdot(2+K\epsilon^{\prime}-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0}))^{2}}\cdot\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S)\cdot\Big(1-(1-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0}))\cdot\exp(-r_{\max}/\lambda_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}^{\star}(S))\Big)\cdot\Delta_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S),

(C.123)

where the first inequality is by the dual representation of the robust expected revenue, the inequality follows the exactly the same argument as (
C.70
), since the left hand side of the inequality shares the same form with the left hand side of (
C.123
).
Here the optimal dual
\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S)
is defined in the same way as (
C.71
), which means that it also enjoys a similar upper and lower bounds we derived in (
C.74
) and (
C.90
)
5
5

5

Again, we remark that the difference in
\epsilon
and
\epsilon^{\prime}
does not change the conclusion of the proofs for (
C.70
) and the bounds of
\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S)
. Besides
\epsilon^{\prime}
, the difference of the classes
\mathcal{V}
and
\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{uni}}
only lies in the items in
\mathcal{N}^{0}
, and it does not make a difference in proving the bounds of
\lambda_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S)
for assortments
S\subseteq[4K]=[N]\setminus\mathcal{N}^{0}
.
.
Specifically, by the conditions on
\rho_{0}
for the uniform revenue instances that

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{1+K\epsilon^{\prime}+N/K}{1+K\epsilon^{\prime}+N/K-\underline{c}_{\rho}}\right)=\log\left(\frac{1+v_{\mathrm{tot}}(\bm{v})}{1+v_{\mathrm{tot}}(\bm{v})-\underline{c}_{\rho}}\right)

(C.124)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\rho_{0}\leq\log\left(\frac{1+v_{\mathrm{tot}}(\bm{v})}{\overline{c}_{\rho}+v_{\mathrm{tot}}(\bm{v})}\right)=\log\left(\frac{1+K\epsilon^{\prime}+N/K}{\overline{c}_{\rho}+K\epsilon^{\prime}+N/K}\right),

(C.125)

we have the that

\displaystyle\frac{r_{\max}}{\log\left(\frac{2}{(c^{\clubsuit}_{\rho}\wedge 1/2)\cdot\log(1+\overline{c}_{\rho})}\right)}\leq\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S)\leq\frac{r_{\max}}{\log\left(\frac{2+K\cdot\epsilon}{2+K\cdot\epsilon-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}\right)},

(C.126)

where
c^{\clubsuit}_{\rho}
is a constant depending on
\overline{c}_{\rho}
,
and consequently that

\displaystyle 1-(1-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0}))\cdot\exp\big(-r_{\max}/\lambda_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}^{\star}(S)\big)\geq\underline{c}_{\rho}\cdot\frac{2+K\epsilon^{\prime}-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}{2},

(C.127)

following the same argument as (
C.104
).
Therefore, we obtain from (
C.123
), (
C.126
), and (
C.127
) that

\displaystyle\inf_{\pi(\cdot):(2^{[N]}\times[N])^{n}\mapsto 2^{[N]}}\sup_{(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{uni}}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{D}\sim\otimes_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{v}}(\cdot|S_{k})}\big[\mathrm{SupOpt}_{\rho(\cdot;\cdot)}(\pi(\mathbb{D});\bm{v},\bm{r})\big]

(C.128)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\inf_{\pi(\cdot):(2^{[N]}\times[N])^{n}\mapsto 2^{[N]}}\sup_{(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{uni}}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{D}\sim\otimes_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{v}}(\cdot|S_{k})}\big[\mathrm{SupOpt}_{\rho(\cdot;\cdot)}(\widetilde{S}(\pi(\mathbb{D}));\bm{v},\bm{r})\big]

(C.129)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\inf_{\pi(\cdot):(2^{[N]}\times[N])^{n}\mapsto 2^{[N]}}\sup_{(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{uni}}}\bigg\{\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{4\cdot(2+K\epsilon^{\prime}-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0}))^{2}}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{D}\sim\otimes_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{v}}(\cdot|S_{k})}\bigg[\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(\widetilde{S}(\pi(\mathbb{D})))

(C.130)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\Big(1-(1-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0}))\cdot\exp\big(-r_{\max}/\lambda_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}^{\star}(\widetilde{S}(\pi(\mathbb{D})))\big)\Big)\cdot\Delta(S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}},\widetilde{S}(\pi(\mathbb{D})))\bigg]\bigg\}

(C.131)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\inf_{S\subseteq[4k],|S|=K}\sup_{(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{uni}}}\bigg\{\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{4\cdot(2+K\epsilon^{\prime}-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0}))^{2}}\cdot\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}(S)\cdot\Big(1-(1-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0}))\cdot\exp\big(-r_{\max}/\lambda_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}^{\star}(S)\big)\Big)\bigg\}

(C.132)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\inf_{\pi(\cdot):(2^{[N]}\times[N])^{n}\mapsto 2^{[N]}}\sup_{(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{uni}}}\bigg\{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{D}\sim\otimes_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{v}}(\cdot|S_{k})}\big[\Delta(S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}},\pi(\mathbb{D}))\big]\bigg\}

(C.133)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\frac{\underline{c}_{\rho}\cdot\epsilon^{\prime}}{8\cdot\log\left(\frac{2}{(c^{\clubsuit}_{\rho}\wedge 1/2)\cdot\log(1+\overline{c}_{\rho})}\right)}\cdot\frac{r_{\max}}{2+K\epsilon^{\prime}-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}

(C.134)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\inf_{\pi(\cdot):(2^{[N]}\times[N])^{n}\mapsto 2^{[N]}}\sup_{(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{uni}}}\bigg\{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{D}\sim\otimes_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{v}}(\cdot|S_{k})}\big[\Delta(S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}},\pi(\mathbb{D}))\big]\bigg\}.

(C.135)

Consequently, it remains to derive the minimax lower bound for the difference
\Delta(S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}},\pi(\mathbb{D}))
for the hard instance class
\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{uni}}
, for which we invoke Lemma
B.8
.
This gives us

\displaystyle\inf_{\pi(\cdot):(2^{[N]}\times[N])^{n}\mapsto 2^{[N]}}\sup_{(\bm{v},\bm{r})\in\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{uni}}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{D}\sim\otimes_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{v}}(\cdot|S_{k})}\big[\mathrm{SupOpt}_{\rho(\cdot;\cdot)}(\pi(\mathbb{D});\bm{v},\bm{r})\big]

(C.136)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\frac{\underline{c}_{\rho}\cdot\sqrt{C_{1}}\cdot\epsilon^{\prime}}{1024\sqrt{5}\log\left(\frac{2}{(c^{\clubsuit}_{\rho}\wedge 1/2)\cdot\log(1+\overline{c}_{\rho})}\right)}\cdot\frac{r_{\max}}{2+K\epsilon^{\prime}-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{K}}{\epsilon^{\prime}\cdot\sqrt{n_{\min}}}

(C.137)

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{\underline{c}_{\rho}\cdot\sqrt{C_{1}}}{1024\sqrt{5}\log\left(\frac{2}{(c^{\clubsuit}_{\rho}\wedge 1/2)\cdot\log(1+\overline{c}_{\rho})}\right)}\cdot\frac{r_{\max}\cdot\sqrt{K}}{2+K\epsilon^{\prime}-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{1}{\min_{i\in S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}}n_{i}}}

(C.138)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\frac{\underline{c}_{\rho}\cdot\sqrt{C_{1}}}{2048\sqrt{10}\log\left(\frac{2}{(c^{\clubsuit}_{\rho}\wedge 1/2)\cdot\log(1+\overline{c}_{\rho})}\right)}\cdot\frac{r_{\max}\cdot\sqrt{K}}{\sqrt{1+(1+K\epsilon^{\prime})/2-a^{\dagger}(\rho_{0})}}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{1}{\min_{i\in S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}}n_{i}}}

(C.139)

\displaystyle\qquad\cong\frac{r_{\max}\cdot\sqrt{K}}{\sqrt{1+\sum_{j\in S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}}v_{j}/2-(1-e^{-\rho_{0}})\cdot(1+v_{\mathrm{tot}}(\bm{v}))}}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{1}{\min_{i\in S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}}n_{i}}},

(C.140)

where the first inequality applies Lemma
B.8
to (
C.135
), and the last inequality hides the global constants and the fact that
\sum_{j\in S^{\star}_{\bm{v},\bm{r}}}v_{j}=1+K\epsilon^{\prime}
. This completes the proof of Theorem
5.4
for uniform revenue case.
∎

Appendix D
Technical Lemmas

Lemma D.1

(Monotonicity under optimal robust assortment (Example
2.1
))
.

Consider Example
2.1
and two sets of parameters
\bm{v}
and
\bm{v}^{\prime}
such that
v_{j}\leq v_{j}^{\prime}
for all
j\in[N]
.
Then for the optimal robust assortment
S^{\star}_{\bm{v}}
associated with
\bm{v}
, it holds that

\displaystyle R_{\rho}(S^{\star}_{\bm{v}};\bm{v})\leq R_{\rho}(S^{\star}_{\bm{v}};\bm{v}^{\prime}).

(D.1)

Proof of Lemma
D.1
.

To facilitate presentation, for any parameter
\bm{v}
, we define

\displaystyle(S^{\star}_{\bm{v}},\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v}})\in\mathop{\mathrm{argmax}}_{S\subseteq[N],|S|\leq K,\lambda\geq 0}H_{\rho}(S,\lambda;\bm{v}),\quad R^{\star}_{\rho}(\bm{v}):=H_{\rho}(S^{\star}_{\bm{v}},\lambda^{\star}_{\bm{v}};\bm{v}),

(D.2)

where we recall that
H_{\rho}(S,\lambda;\bm{v})
is defined as

\displaystyle H_{\rho}(S,\lambda;\bm{v})=-\lambda\cdot\log\left(\frac{\sum_{i\in S_{+}}v_{i}\cdot\exp(-r_{i}/\lambda)}{\sum_{i\in S_{+}}v_{i}}\right)-\lambda\cdot\rho.

(D.3)

Then by Proposition
2.1
, proving Lemma
D.1
is equivalent to showing that

\displaystyle\sup_{\lambda\geq 0}H_{\rho}(S^{\star}_{\bm{v}},\lambda;\bm{v})\leq\sup_{\lambda\geq 0}H_{\rho}(S^{\star}_{\bm{v}},\lambda;\bm{v}^{\prime}).

(D.4)

To this end, consider the following upper bound,
